# Research Findings Registry

> **1034 key findings from 75 research sessions.** Each finding keeps the session's own evidence grade (EGP-1.0: A/B measured or primary; C small-sample, vendor or practitioner; D judgement) and the exact line where it is stated. Data: [data/findings.json](../data/findings.json). Sessions are dated snapshots; known errors are listed in [ERRATA.md](ERRATA.md).

**Grades:** A 343 · B 417 · C 129 · D 12 · E 6 · M 1 · ungraded 126.

| Finding | Grade | Statement |

| [T0-04.F1](sessions/T0-04-ats-filtering-funnel-conversion-math.md) L58 | A | The ">75% auto-rejected" statistic is unsupported as stated; deterministic rejection exists, but it is employer-configured and keyed to form answers, not resume prose |

| [T0-04.F5](sessions/T0-04-ats-filtering-funnel-conversion-math.md) L74 | C | Two-column "garbling" is real but extractor-dependent; no single open-source strategy survives all layouts [Local] |
| [T0-04.F6](sessions/T0-04-ats-filtering-funnel-conversion-math.md) L78 | C | LaTeX PDFs are not inherently unparseable; failures come from the font-encoding pipeline [Local] |

| [T0-22.F1](sessions/T0-22-referrals-warm-intros-cold-outreach-law.md) L72 | B | Per application, vouched referrals convert far better than inbound: 40% vs 3% to interview and 16% vs 6% from interview to offer. By my arithmetic that is about 6.4 offers per 100 referred applications vs 0.18 per 100 inbound (≈36×); sourced is 1.5 per 100 (≈8×). Gem reports referrals at 11× inbound. The data is descriptive, not causal |
| [T0-22.F2](sessions/T0-22-referrals-warm-intros-cold-outreach-law.md) L73 | B/C/E | Referrals are about 18% of hires from about 1% of applications; inbound is 43–52% of hires; technical roles skew to sourced hires (24% vs 18%). The 70–85% "hidden market" traces to a 1966 claim and a 2016 self-selected poll. Household surveys of contact use (one-third to two-thirds of jobs) measure a different thing |
| [T0-22.F3](sessions/T0-22-referrals-warm-intros-cold-outreach-law.md) L74 | A/B | Peer-reviewed work splits what vendor data merges. (a) Selection: who gets referred. (b) Information: referrals reveal performance and persistence not visible on a CV. (c) Team effects: referrals do better working with their referrer. Lower quit rates recur. Wage premia fade with tenure, and jobs found through family and friends carry a 5–7% wage discount in two datasets. The causal effect of "con |
| [T0-22.F4](sessions/T0-22-referrals-warm-intros-cold-outreach-law.md) L75 | B | Incentives shape who is referred. With flat pay, referrers named closer and weaker people (Kolkata lab-in-field). Larger bonuses raised referral volume but lowered quality in an RCT, while the programme cut attrition by 15%. Bonus-driven referrals of strangers deserve a discount |
| [T0-22.F5](sessions/T0-22-referrals-warm-intros-cold-outreach-law.md) L76 | B | Weak ties matter causally in tech. Randomised experiments on about 20M LinkedIn users (2015–2019) found most job mobility came from moderately weak ties (about 10 mutual connections), with larger effects in digital industries. The authors are LinkedIn-affiliated |
| [T0-22.F6](sessions/T0-22-referrals-warm-intros-cold-outreach-law.md) L77 | A/B | In an ATS a referral is a source or origin tag plus referrer attribution. Treatment of someone who already applied is employer-specific. GitLab downgrades a referral to an endorsement, with no bonus, once talent acquisition has progressed the application. Same-function referrals interview more (37% vs 26%) and are hired more (5.2% vs 3.1%) in business roles |
| [T0-22.F7](sessions/T0-22-referrals-warm-intros-cold-outreach-law.md) L78 | A/C | Strangers: GitLab tells employees to decline cold referral requests and excludes strangers from referral status. A self-selected survey (n≈500) rated cold referrals net negative at FAANG-type firms and small startups. Paid referral markets exist, with no outcome evidence |
| [T0-22.F8](sessions/T0-22-referrals-warm-intros-cold-outreach-law.md) L79 | B/C/E | No measured candidate-initiated reply or interview rate exists in anything I could open. Proxies: Instantly 3.43% average, 5.5% top quartile, 10.7% top decile (sales email); Pin 4.96% automated and 6.31% recruiter-written email, and 17.08% LinkedIn (recruiter-to-candidate); Huntr trackers 1.95% of 1.24M applications reached interview. Claims of 15–50% for job-seeker cold email trace to vendor blog |
| [T0-22.F9](sessions/T0-22-referrals-warm-intros-cold-outreach-law.md) L80 | — | The 60-send stop rule: 0 replies in 60 bounds the true rate below 4.9% (one-sided 95%). At a true 3% there is still a 16% chance of zero replies in 60. At 3.43% there is an 88% chance of at least one reply. So 60 sends detects rates of 5% or more but cannot separate 0–3% |
| [T0-22.F10](sessions/T0-22-referrals-warm-intros-cold-outreach-law.md) L81 | A/B/D | The statutes reviewed regulate "direct marketing" (EU, UK), "commercial advertisement or promotion" (US), "commercial activity" (Canada), "commercial electronic messages" (Australia) or bulk commercial messages (Singapore). The data is descriptive, not causal |
| [T0-22.F2](sessions/T0-22-referrals-warm-intros-cold-outreach-law.md) L73 | B/C/E | Referrals are about 18% of hires from about 1% of applications; inbound is 43–52% of hires; technical roles skew to sourced hires (24% vs 18%). The 70–85% "hidden market" traces to a 1966 claim and a 2016 self-selected poll. Household surveys of contact use (one-third to two-thirds of jobs) measure a different thing |
| [T0-22.F3](sessions/T0-22-referrals-warm-intros-cold-outreach-law.md) L74 | A/B | Peer-reviewed work splits what vendor data merges. (a) Selection: who gets referred. (b) Information: referrals reveal performance and persistence not visible on a CV. (c) Team effects: referrals do better working with their referrer. Lower quit rates recur. Wage premia fade with tenure, and jobs found through family and friends carry a 5–7% wage discount in two datasets. The causal effect of "con |
| [T0-22.F4](sessions/T0-22-referrals-warm-intros-cold-outreach-law.md) L75 | B | Incentives shape who is referred. With flat pay, referrers named closer and weaker people (Kolkata lab-in-field). Larger bonuses raised referral volume but lowered quality in an RCT, while the programme cut attrition by 15%. Bonus-driven referrals of strangers deserve a discount |
| [T0-22.F5](sessions/T0-22-referrals-warm-intros-cold-outreach-law.md) L76 | B | Weak ties matter causally in tech. Randomised experiments on about 20M LinkedIn users (2015–2019) found most job mobility came from moderately weak ties (about 10 mutual connections), with larger effects in digital industries. The authors are LinkedIn-affiliated |
| [T0-22.F6](sessions/T0-22-referrals-warm-intros-cold-outreach-law.md) L77 | A/B | In an ATS a referral is a source or origin tag plus referrer attribution. Treatment of someone who already applied is employer-specific. GitLab downgrades a referral to an endorsement, with no bonus, once talent acquisition has progressed the application. Same-function referrals interview more (37% vs 26%) and are hired more (5.2% vs 3.1%) in business roles |
| [T0-22.F7](sessions/T0-22-referrals-warm-intros-cold-outreach-law.md) L78 | A/C | Strangers: GitLab tells employees to decline cold referral requests and excludes strangers from referral status. A self-selected survey (n≈500) rated cold referrals net negative at FAANG-type firms and small startups. Paid referral markets exist, with no outcome evidence |
| [T0-22.F8](sessions/T0-22-referrals-warm-intros-cold-outreach-law.md) L79 | B/C/E | No measured candidate-initiated reply or interview rate exists in anything I could open. Proxies: Instantly 3.43% average, 5.5% top quartile, 10.7% top decile (sales email); Pin 4.96% automated and 6.31% recruiter-written email, and 17.08% LinkedIn (recruiter-to-candidate); Huntr trackers 1.95% of 1.24M applications reached interview. Claims of 15–50% for job-seeker cold email trace to vendor blog |
| [T0-22.F9](sessions/T0-22-referrals-warm-intros-cold-outreach-law.md) L80 | — | The 60-send stop rule: 0 replies in 60 bounds the true rate below 4.9% (one-sided 95%). At a true 3% there is still a 16% chance of zero replies in 60. At 3.43% there is an 88% chance of at least one reply. So 60 sends detects rates of 5% or more but cannot separate 0–3% |
| [T0-22.F10](sessions/T0-22-referrals-warm-intros-cold-outreach-law.md) L81 | A/B/D | The statutes reviewed regulate "direct marketing" (EU, UK), "commercial advertisement or promotion" (US), "commercial activity" (Canada), "commercial electronic messages" (Australia) or bulk commercial messages (Singapore). A personal request for work probably falls outside by definition, but no regulator says so. The GDPR household exemption is read narrowly. Canada exempts the first message afte |
| [T0-22.F11](sessions/T0-22-referrals-warm-intros-cold-outreach-law.md) L82 | C/B | Contact data: hit rates were 29.8–55.2% in a vendor-run audit of 1.3M lookups, against vendor claims of 92–99%, and a hit is not a deliverable address. CNIL fined Kaspr €240,000 for collecting LinkedIn contact details users had restricted. LinkedIn bans bots and scrapers and has sued ProAPIs and Proxycurl |
| [T0-22.F12](sessions/T0-22-referrals-warm-intros-cold-outreach-law.md) L83 | B/C | Recruiters are paid by employers (contingency 15–25% of base; retained 25–35% of total compensation); candidates never pay. A payment demand is a scam marker. FTC-reported losses rose from $90M to $501M (2020–2024). India's NCS advisory is dated 2026-11-09 |
| [T0-22.F13](sessions/T0-22-referrals-warm-intros-cold-outreach-law.md) L84 | B/C | India: services firms pay referral bonuses (TCS ₹40,000; Infosys ₹10,000–50,000 by level). GCC hiring is projected at 510,452 jobs in 2026, with 64% needing AI or data skills. No measured Indian referral share exists. Paid referral groups and fraud alerts both exist |
| [T0-22.F14](sessions/T0-22-referrals-warm-intros-cold-outreach-law.md) L85 | — | Yield per hour is unmeasured. Illustratively, referral beats cold applying if you can secure a vouched referral in under about 12 hours (about 6 hours if half the lift is selection). Artefact outreach, community and writing are unmeasured |

### Stage 10: Onboarding (27)

| Finding | Grade | Statement |
|---|:---:|---|
| [T0-09.F1](sessions/T0-09-onboarding-probation-first-90-days.md) L60 | — | The "up to 35%" claim has no traceable source; the nearest published numbers measure different things |
| [T0-09.F2](sessions/T0-09-onboarding-probation-first-90-days.md) L73 | B | "AI Expectations Gap" is a descriptive label, not an established construct. The mechanism is documented, but the headline failure rates are soft |
| [T0-09.F3](sessions/T0-09-onboarding-probation-first-90-days.md) L79 | B | At the individual level, early failure looks like role/expectation mismatch plus thin onboarding. AI-specific measurement is missing |
| [T0-09.F4](sessions/T0-09-onboarding-probation-first-90-days.md) L86 | B | Data problems are among the most-cited blockers, and "no usable data" is a common starting state, not an exception |
| [T0-09.F5](sessions/T0-09-onboarding-probation-first-90-days.md) L91 | A | Compute and budget are gated by process and usage history. The levers are documented; the "negotiation tactics" are inference |
| [T0-09.F6](sessions/T0-09-onboarding-probation-first-90-days.md) L97 | A | Non-determinism is measurable and partly engineering-fixable, and the engineer who quantifies it first controls the narrative |
| [T0-09.F7](sessions/T0-09-onboarding-probation-first-90-days.md) L102 | A | Governance gates are real and moving in 2026, and two common assumptions are wrong |
| [T0-09.F8](sessions/T0-09-onboarding-probation-first-90-days.md) L109 | B/C | Public, named GenAI probation rubrics essentially do not exist. The observable standards are job-posting deliverables, onboarding metrics, AI-fluency criteria and probation mechanics |
| [T0-09.F9](sessions/T0-09-onboarding-probation-first-90-days.md) L116 | B | Startup-vs-GCC differences are plausible but thinly evidenced; the GCC evidence is about aggregates |
| [T0-09.F10](sessions/T0-09-onboarding-probation-first-90-days.md) L122 | A | Two quick wins have independent support (a small regression eval suite built from real failures, and measured cost cuts). "~40%" is plausible, workload-dependent and must be measured on your own traffic |
| [T0-09.F11](sessions/T0-09-onboarding-probation-first-90-days.md) L127 | B/C | Engineering-analytics platforms double as evaluation instruments on you, and productivity evidence is contested |
| [T0-09.F12](sessions/T0-09-onboarding-probation-first-90-days.md) L132 | B | Case library: failures share a shape. Early demo success, an inflated promise, no eval, guardrail or owner, then reversal or liability |
| [T0-71.F1](sessions/T0-71-onboarding-growth-ai-roles.md) L62 | B | Role clarity is one of four adjustment indicators linked to newcomer outcomes. Plans should cover relationships as well as criteria |
| [T0-71.F2](sessions/T0-71-onboarding-growth-ai-roles.md) L63 | — | A misunderstood or miscommunicated problem is the first of five root causes of AI project failure. Scope excluded prompt-only LLM projects |
| [T0-71.F3](sessions/T0-71-onboarding-growth-ai-roles.md) L64 | A | No public, named GenAI probation rubric exists. Published AI-role artefacts define levels, not probation |
| [T0-71.F4](sessions/T0-71-onboarding-growth-ai-roles.md) L65 | B | Employers write AI use into expectations. Thresholds are unpublished |
| [T0-71.F5](sessions/T0-71-onboarding-growth-ai-roles.md) L66 | B | Measured AI coding gains run from slower to much faster, and gains attenuate before release |
| [T0-71.F6](sessions/T0-71-onboarding-growth-ai-roles.md) L67 | — | No evidence supports a "10× engineer with AI" |
| [T0-71.F7](sessions/T0-71-onboarding-growth-ai-roles.md) L68 | B | AI use can lower comprehension while learning |
| [T0-71.F8](sessions/T0-71-onboarding-growth-ai-roles.md) L69 | C | No probation-outcome or first-90-day attrition data exist for AI roles. India signals are mixed |
| [T0-71.F9](sessions/T0-71-onboarding-growth-ai-roles.md) L70 | B/C | India has no central probation cap. The contract governs, and the Labour Codes are in force |
| [T0-71.F10](sessions/T0-71-onboarding-growth-ai-roles.md) L71 | B | Promotion rates and raises depend on definition |
| [T0-71.F11](sessions/T0-71-onboarding-growth-ai-roles.md) L72 | C | AI promotion speed is measured by one vendor only |
| [T0-71.F12](sessions/T0-71-onboarding-growth-ai-roles.md) L73 | C/B | Management tracks are narrowing and junior pipelines are shrinking |
| [T0-71.F13](sessions/T0-71-onboarding-growth-ai-roles.md) L74 | B/A | Long hours and AI-driven intensification carry measurable health and burnout risk. AI-role-specific data are absent |
| [T0-71.F14](sessions/T0-71-onboarding-growth-ai-roles.md) L75 | B | First-90-day practices have supporting evidence. The "quick wins" are practice-curated |

| [T0-67.F13](sessions/T0-67-transitions-devops-pm-it-services-students.md) L64 | B | Cost-adjusted returns to graduate degrees are small for computer science (+6%), computer engineering (+2%) and electrical or mechanical engineering (+4%), against +17% on average and +41% for law. US master's holders earn a median of about USD 15,500 a year more than bachelor's holders |
| [T0-67.F14](sessions/T0-67-transitions-devops-pm-it-services-students.md) L65 | B | Internships are the best-measured entry route: 63.1% of 2024–25 interns were converted, with 88.3% acceptance. In-person interns received offers at 71.9%, against 56.2% for hybrid interns (2023–24 cohort) |
| [T0-67.F15](sessions/T0-67-transitions-devops-pm-it-services-students.md) L66 | B | Post-study rules are moving. The US has a proposed OPT fee (comments closed 2026-11-09 per tracker), a proposed USD 103,265 H-1B fee, and a USD 100,000 fee blocked by court order. The UK Graduate Route falls to 18 months from 2027. Canada froze PGWP fields for 2026 and exempts master's and PhD students from the study-permit cap. Outcome evidence is mixed: student-visa arrivals earn a median USD 11 |
| [T2-01.F1](sessions/T2-01-tier-calibrated-execution-playbooks.md) L73 | B/C/D | Only 8–12 of the 30 persona×tier permutations represent high-volume transition corridors; the remainder are either implausible (Non-Tech → Tier 5), already-occupied (PhD at Tier 3), or require a two-stage transition that exceeds 12 months |
| [T2-01.F2](sessions/T2-01-tier-calibrated-execution-playbooks.md) L74 | B/C/D | The 12-stage funnel compresses to 5–7 active stages per persona because experienced practitioners bypass Awareness/Exploration, credentialed practitioners skip Certification, and senior pivots compress Skills + Portfolio into a single build phase |
| [T2-01.F3](sessions/T2-01-tier-calibrated-execution-playbooks.md) L75 | B | Evaluation is the universal gate across all 5 personas and all viable tiers — it appears in portfolio (Stage 5), interview execution (Stage 9–10), and onboarding (Stage 12). Each persona brings a different comparative advantage to eval work |
| [T2-01.F4](sessions/T2-01-tier-calibrated-execution-playbooks.md) L76 | A/B | Tax/legal structure is a one-way door that must be decided at Stage 6 (Discovery/Application), not Stage 11 (Negotiation) — the ₹75L §44ADA cliff (T0-07) and O-1A evidence-building timeline (T0-08) both require 12–18 months of preparation that cannot be retrofitted |
| [T2-01.F5](sessions/T2-01-tier-calibrated-execution-playbooks.md) L77 | B/C | The interview funnel bifurcates into infrastructure and application tracks (T1-03 KF2), and the correct track depends on persona×tier: Backend→Tier 3+ is infrastructure-heavy; Frontend→Tier 2 is application-heavy; PhD→Tier 4+ straddles both. Preparing for the wrong track is the most avoidable failure mode |
| [T2-01.F6](sessions/T2-01-tier-calibrated-execution-playbooks.md) L78 | A/B/C | First-90-day failure modes are persona-specific and predictable from upstream findings: role ambiguity (43% of all hires; T0-09 F3), data absence (63% of orgs; T0-09 F4), compute gating (85% delayed; T0-09 F5), and non-determinism surprise (up to 15% accuracy variance; T0-09 F6) |
| [T2-01.F7](sessions/T2-01-tier-calibrated-execution-playbooks.md) L79 | B/C | The freelance/fractional path (T0-18) diverges from the employment funnel at Stage 6 and requires a parallel playbook spine: network-based acquisition replaces job boards, MSA/contract structure replaces offer negotiation, and client retention replaces onboarding |
| [T2-01.F8](sessions/T2-01-tier-calibrated-execution-playbooks.md) L80 | A/B | Geographic arbitrage (India→US remote) at Tier 2–3 is the highest-ROI corridor but requires navigating three simultaneous regulatory regimes (Indian tax, US client onboarding, and optionally visa/relocation), each with its own timeline and cliff effects |
| [T2-01.F9](sessions/T2-01-tier-calibrated-execution-playbooks.md) L81 | B/C/D | Portfolio calibration must match the target tier: Tier 0–1 needs one evaluated end-to-end system; Tier 2–3 needs two systems with different failure surfaces plus CI-gated evals; Tier 4–5 needs published research, industry-visible contributions, and evidence meeting O-1A or equivalent standards |
| [T2-01.F10](sessions/T2-01-tier-calibrated-execution-playbooks.md) L82 | D | No longitudinal outcome data exists for any persona×tier corridor (T0-06 KF9). Every timeline in this document is a transparent model, not measured data. The playbooks are planning frameworks calibrated against the few available anchors (an 8-month frontend retrospective, Huntr's 82–108 day search medians, and vendor reference classes) |

<a id="method"></a>
### Method, tooling and repository design (50)

| Finding | Grade | Statement |
|---|:---:|---|
| [T0-10.F1](sessions/T0-10-career-tooling-ecosystem-12-stage-funnel.md) L61 | A | Bot and spam defenses exist at all three named ATSs, but they are employer-configured, signal-based and scoped to one employer |
| [T0-10.F2](sessions/T0-10-career-tooling-ecosystem-12-stage-funnel.md) L64 | A/B | The injected claim ("auto-apply bots trigger automated IP blacklists and ATS shadow-bans") is partly right and mostly wrong as stated |
| [T0-10.F3](sessions/T0-10-career-tooling-ecosystem-12-stage-funnel.md) L67 | B/C | Automated-browser submissions were demonstrably rejected by Ashby in at least one reproducible report; the mechanism is inferred, not confirmed |
| [T0-10.F4](sessions/T0-10-career-tooling-ecosystem-12-stage-funnel.md) L70 | B/C | Auto-apply at volume has weak and mostly adverse evidence, and "Simplify auto-apply" needs correcting |
| [T0-10.F5](sessions/T0-10-career-tooling-ecosystem-12-stage-funnel.md) L73 | A | AI writing help can raise hiring odds in narrow forms; generic AI text erodes the signal |
| [T0-10.F6](sessions/T0-10-career-tooling-ecosystem-12-stage-funnel.md) L76 | C | No independent ATS-parser benchmark of consumer resume builders exists in what I found; vendor "ATS scores" are internal heuristics, and "the ATS rejects you" is mostly a knockout-question and recruiter-triage story |
| [T0-10.F7](sessions/T0-10-career-tooling-ecosystem-12-stage-funnel.md) L79 | B/C | The resume and career-builder category carries billing dark-pattern and data-exposure risk |
| [T0-10.F8](sessions/T0-10-career-tooling-ecosystem-12-stage-funnel.md) L82 | B/C | Live AI copilots are high-risk; employer policy is diverging; detection figures are vendor-sourced |
| [T0-10.F9](sessions/T0-10-career-tooling-ecosystem-12-stage-funnel.md) L85 | B | For GenAI system design the best-verifiable prep offering is Hello Interview; no prep platform has independent outcome evidence |
| [T0-10.F10](sessions/T0-10-career-tooling-ecosystem-12-stage-funnel.md) L88 | B/C | Comp data is usable for base and big-tech total comp, but equity values (especially private-company options) are not standardized, and no tool has verified accuracy against realized outcomes |
| [T0-10.F11](sessions/T0-10-career-tooling-ecosystem-12-stage-funnel.md) L91 | A/B | The discovery and execution layers are polluted by scams, ghost jobs and fake-recruiter malware |
| [T0-10.F12](sessions/T0-10-career-tooling-ecosystem-12-stage-funnel.md) L94 | A/B | Platform stability and lock-in risk is real at the skills, portfolio and extension layers |
| [T0-26.F1](sessions/T0-26-job-search-tools-agents-oss-mcp.md) L49 | B | LinkedIn and Indeed prohibit third-party automation of applying. LinkedIn's help page bars bots and extensions that automate activity on its site. Indeed's India help page lists third-party bots that apply for jobs as prohibited conduct, and its terms bar automating Indeed Apply outside its own tooling |
| [T0-26.F2](sessions/T0-26-job-search-tools-agents-oss-mcp.md) L50 | A/C | Indeed tested its own "Apply For Me" from 2026-07-07, then on 2026-08-04 paused the automatic mode and kept a mode where the seeker reviews every draft. Trade press attributes the pause to employer pushback |
| [T0-26.F3](sessions/T0-26-job-search-tools-agents-oss-mcp.md) L51 | B/C | No independent controlled study of auto-apply outcomes was found. Huntr's correlational data (to 2026-10-01): 8.0% of applications led to interviews at 11–20 applications versus 2.4% at 100+, yet 72.0% of heavy appliers got at least one interview versus 47.8%. Of 68 survey users of send-for-you tools, 9% reported more interviews, 49% the same, 25% fewer |
| [T0-26.F4](sessions/T0-26-job-search-tools-agents-oss-mcp.md) L52 | A/B | Employer systems have countermeasures. Greenhouse Real Talent flags bot and bulk-tool spam (phased in from Q3 2025). Ashby documents duplicate merging and Application Limits. About 1% of 196,682 résumés on one hiring platform carried hidden prompt injections |
| [T0-26.F5](sessions/T0-26-job-search-tools-agents-oss-mcp.md) L53 | A | Simplify bundles two behaviours in one account. Copilot autofills and the user submits. Autopilot (20 credits a week) submits with no review step and stops rather than submit an answer it cannot determine |
| [T0-26.F6](sessions/T0-26-job-search-tools-agents-oss-mcp.md) L54 | B/C | Jobscan Auto Apply is real: review by default, optional auto-submit, Lever and Workable at launch, bundled in Premium ($49.95 a month). No outcome evidence |
| [T0-26.F7](sessions/T0-26-job-search-tools-agents-oss-mcp.md) L55 | A/B/C | Teal+ costs $13 a week, $29 a month or $79 a quarter (not $9 a week). Huntr Pro is $40 a month, $90 a quarter or $160 per six months. Two third parties report Simplify+ at $19.99 a week, $39.99 a month and $89.99 a quarter. Free tiers cover tracking |
| [T0-26.F8](sessions/T0-26-job-search-tools-agents-oss-mcp.md) L56 | A/B/C | Official MCP servers exist but mostly serve employers. Indeed's is a beta, Claude-only, read-only server for seekers. Greenhouse (open beta, July 2026) and Workable (94 tools by 2026-07-20) serve recruiters and admins. No official LinkedIn job-search MCP was found |
| [T0-26.F9](sessions/T0-26-job-search-tools-agents-oss-mcp.md) L57 | A/B | Community LinkedIn and Indeed MCP servers work through scraping or your logged-in session. The leading one (4,202 stars, Apache-2.0) can import browser session cookies and can message and connect on your behalf |
| [T0-26.F10](sessions/T0-26-job-search-tools-agents-oss-mcp.md) L58 | A/B/C | Official job-data APIs for individuals exist: Adzuna, USAJOBS, Reed (UK only), Jooble, Remotive, Arbeitnow, Careerjet (publisher-oriented), and unauthenticated Greenhouse, Lever and Ashby board endpoints. Labour-market data is US-centred: BLS API v2 (500 queries a day), ONET Web Services, and Indeed Hiring Lab data with no India series. Naukri has no public API |
| [T0-26.F11](sessions/T0-26-job-search-tools-agents-oss-mcp.md) L59 | A | Open-source leaders as of 2026-11-13: career-ops (82,242 stars, never submits), AIHawk (32,498, auto-applies), Reactive Resume (45,680), Resume-Matcher (29,270). OpenResume has had no commit since 2024-10-29 |
| [T0-26.F12](sessions/T0-26-job-search-tools-agents-oss-mcp.md) L60 | B/C | ChatGPT Atlas was discontinued on 2026-08-09 and folded into ChatGPT. Comet and the Claude Chrome extension appear in HUMAN Security's May 2026 agent-traffic data. No board checked sanctions browser agents for applying, and trade press reports agent blocking rates rising |
| [T0-26.F13](sessions/T0-26-job-search-tools-agents-oss-mcp.md) L61 | B | Resume and applicant-data exposures from misconfigured cloud storage hit TalentHook (about 26M files), LiveCareer (5.1M) and HireClick (5.7M) in 2025. Naukri had an API bug exposing recruiter emails |
| [T0-26.F14](sessions/T0-26-job-search-tools-agents-oss-mcp.md) L62 | C | Checker scores diverge by tool (one vendor test: 21% to 80% for the same résumé) and no outcome link exists; use them as gap lists |
| [T0-26.F15](sessions/T0-26-job-search-tools-agents-oss-mcp.md) L63 | B | Non-generative writing assistance raised hires by 8% and wages by 10% in a field experiment on one online labour market (abstract only; sample size not re-verified). LLM salary advice differs by persona, including in audits of 2026-era models |
| [T1-01.F1](sessions/T1-01-repository-information-architecture.md) L58 | B | The Hybrid Spine Architecture is the only model that survives all three test user journeys without duplication or dead ends |
| [T1-01.F2](sessions/T1-01-repository-information-architecture.md) L62 | B | Three directory levels (root → category → topic) is the hard maximum; two levels suffice for most content |
| [T1-01.F3](sessions/T1-01-repository-information-architecture.md) L66 | C | Cross-cutting indices should be static Markdown files with manual curation, not auto-generated, for the first 12 months |
| [T1-01.F4](sessions/T1-01-repository-information-architecture.md) L70 | C | The Diátaxis framework maps imperfectly onto career lifecycle content; a modified taxonomy works better |
| [T1-01.F5](sessions/T1-01-repository-information-architecture.md) L74 | B | The 12-stage funnel from T0-10 requires consolidation to 10 directories for the repository spine |
| [T1-01.F6](sessions/T1-01-repository-information-architecture.md) L78 | A | The upstream role taxonomy (T0-01) defines 10 verified roles and 9 quarantined speculative titles; the directory should index only verified roles |
| [T1-01.F7](sessions/T1-01-repository-information-architecture.md) L82 | B | The 6-tier compensation grid from T0-02 is the correct cross-cutting axis for compensation, but tier boundaries need geographic variants |
| [T1-01.F8](sessions/T1-01-repository-information-architecture.md) L86 | B | Frontmatter metadata must include 12 fields to support cross-referencing, evidence governance (T1-02), and temporal freshness tracking |
| [T1-01.F9](sessions/T1-01-repository-information-architecture.md) L90 | C | MkDocs Material remains the strongest SSG choice for this repository, but the ecosystem transition must be monitored |
| [T1-01.F10](sessions/T1-01-repository-information-architecture.md) L94 | C | markdownlint-cli2 is the correct linting tool; remark-lint is over-engineered for this repository's needs |
| [T1-01.F11](sessions/T1-01-repository-information-architecture.md) L98 | C | Three user navigation personas validate the hybrid architecture; all reach their target content within 2 clicks from the root README |
| [T1-01.F12](sessions/T1-01-repository-information-architecture.md) L102 | C | Non-linear resources (legal contracts, tax calculators, interview question banks) belong in the stage directory closest to their point of use, not in a separate appendices/ catch-all |
| [T1-02.F2](sessions/T1-02-evidence-grounding-protocol.md) L51 | B | Link rot is fast, and the archive layer has its own failure modes |
| [T1-02.F3](sessions/T1-02-evidence-grounding-protocol.md) L53 | B | Decay is real but heterogeneous: separate a fact's instance from its trend |
| [T1-02.F4](sessions/T1-02-evidence-grounding-protocol.md) L55 | B | Policy status can change every few weeks while policy text sits still |
| [T1-02.F5](sessions/T1-02-evidence-grounding-protocol.md) L57 | A | Grade-A official statistics have gaps, vintages and definitional drift |
| [T1-02.F6](sessions/T1-02-evidence-grounding-protocol.md) L59 | B | Salary sources measure different constructs |
| [T1-02.F7](sessions/T1-02-evidence-grounding-protocol.md) L61 | A | One paper can carry three headline numbers; pin versions |
| [T1-02.F8](sessions/T1-02-evidence-grounding-protocol.md) L63 | B | Productivity "contradictions" are mostly differences of scope |
| [T1-02.F9](sessions/T1-02-evidence-grounding-protocol.md) L65 | C | "95% of AI pilots fail" is a case study in provenance decay |
| [T1-02.F10](sessions/T1-02-evidence-grounding-protocol.md) L67 | B | "AI will write 90% of code" is three different claims |
| [T1-02.F11](sessions/T1-02-evidence-grounding-protocol.md) L69 | B | LLM-fabricated citations are measured, and LLM verifiers are unreliable |
| [T1-02.F12](sessions/T1-02-evidence-grounding-protocol.md) L71 | B | Two caps are grounded in evidence and in law |
